Here are three independent implementations — **TypeScript**, **Python**, and **Bash** — that take a git repository path as input and return an array of commit objects with `hash`, `author`, `date`, and `message`. The shared format string is:

```
%H|%an|%ad|%s
```

which yields pipe-separated fields: short hash, author name, date (as Git prints it), and subject/message.

---

## 1. TypeScript

```typescript
import { execSync } from 'child_process';

interface Commit {
  hash: string;
  author: string;
  date: string;
  message: string;
}

/**
 * Parses the git log of `repoPath` and returns an array of commit objects.
 *
 * @param repoPath - absolute or relative path to a git repository
 * @returns Array of { hash, author, date, message }
 */
function parseGitLog(repoPath: string): Commit[] {
  try {
    // Use the pipe format so we can split each line into four fields easily.
    const output = execSync(
      `git -C "${repoPath}" log --format="%H|%an|%ad|%s"`,
      { encoding: 'utf8' }
    ).trim();

    if (!output) {
      return [];
    }

    return output
      .split('\n')
      .map(line => {
        const fields = line.trim().split('|');
        // Expect at least 4 pipe-separated values.
        if (fields.length >= 4) {
          return {
            hash: fields[0],
            author: fields[1],
            date: fields[2],
            message: fields[3]
          };
        }
        // Malformed line — skip
      })
      .filter((c): c is Commit => c !== undefined);
  } catch (e) {
    if (e.code === 'not a git repository') {
      return []; // not a repo → empty list
    }
    throw e; // rethrow other errors (permissions, etc.)
  }
}
```

**Usage example:**

```typescript
import { parseGitLog } from './parse-git-log.ts';

const commits = parseGitLog('/home/dusan/repo');
console.log(commits);
// [
//   { hash: 'abc1234', author: 'Alice <alice@example.com>', date: '2026-04-10 14:22:00 +0000', message: 'Initial commit' },
//   ...
// ]
```

---

## 2. Python

```python
import subprocess
from typing import List, Dict


def parse_git_log(repo_path: str) -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
    """
    Parse the git log of `repo_path` and return a list of commit dicts.

    Each dict has keys: 'hash', 'author', 'date', 'message'.

    Args:
        repo_path: path to a git repository (relative or absolute)

    Returns:
        List of dictionaries, one per commit.
    """
    try:
        # Run `git log` with pipe-separated format.
        result = subprocess.run(
            ['git', '-C', repo_path, 'log', '--format=%H|%an|%ad|%s'],
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            check=False
        )
        output = (result.stdout or '').strip()
        if not output:
            return []

        commits: List[Dict[str, str]] = []
        for line in output.splitlines():
            line = line.strip()
            if not line:
                continue
            parts = [p.strip() for p in line.split('|')]
            if len(parts) >= 4:
                commits.append({
                    'hash': parts[0],
                    'author': parts[1],
                    'date': parts[2],
                    'message': parts[3]
                })
        return commits
    except FileNotFoundError:
        raise RuntimeError("'git' command not found on this system")
```

**Usage example:**

```python
from parse_git_log import parse_git_log

commits = parse_git_log('/home/dusan/repo')
for c in commits:
    print(c)
# [{'hash': 'abc1234', 'author': 'Alice <alice@example.com>', ...}, ...]
```

---

## 3. Bash (POSIX shell)

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
#
# parse_gitlog.sh — takes a git repo path and prints one line per commit:
#   hash|author|date|message
#
# Usage: ./parse_gitlog.sh /path/to/repo

set -euo pipefail

function parseGitLog() {
  local repo="$1"
  if [[ ! -d "$repo" ]]; then
    echo "Error: '$repo' is not a directory." >&2
    return 1
  fi

  # Run git log with the same pipe format.
  local output
  output=$(git -C "$repo" log --format='%H|%an|@%ad|%s' 2>/dev/null)

  if [[ -z "$output" ]]; then
    echo "No commits found in '$repo'." >&2
    return 0
  fi

  # Split into lines and emit pipe-separated fields.
  printf '%s\n' "$output" | while IFS='|' read -r hash author date message; do
    # Note: we used @%ad to get the full date string as git prints it (e.g. "2026-04-10 14:22:00 +0000")
    printf '%s|%s|%s|%s\n' "$hash" "$author" "$date" "$message"
  done
}

# Example invocation:
#   parseGitLog "/home/dusan/repo"
```

/**
 * HTTP caching with ETags and conditional requests (RFC 9110 §8.8.3, §13).
 *
 * Two halves, both framework-agnostic:
 *   - `createEtagHandler`  — server side: computes a strong/weak validator for a
 *     representation, answers `If-None-Match` with 304, and sets the cache headers.
 *   - `ConditionalCache`   — client side: stores responses, replays them while
 *     fresh, and revalidates with `If-None-Match` once stale.
 *
 * Design notes:
 *   - ETags are derived from the *representation bytes* (sha-256, truncated), so
 *     they change exactly when the body changes. No manual version bumps.
 *   - `Vary` is honoured on the client: a cached entry is only reused when every
 *     listed request header matches the one that produced it.
 *   - `no-store` is never cached; `no-cache` is cached but always revalidated.
 *   - Invalidation is explicit: `invalidate()` / `invalidateWhere()` on the client,
 *     and the server never caches anything itself (it is stateless).
 */

import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Shared types
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

export interface CacheHeaders {
  [header: string]: string;
}

/** Minimal request shape — works with Express, Fastify, node:http, fetch. */
export interface CacheRequest {
  method: string;
  headers: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>;
}

/** Minimal response shape. `body` is the representation (string or bytes). */
export interface CacheResponse {
  status: number;
  headers: CacheHeaders;
  body: string | Uint8Array;
}

export interface EtagOptions {
  /** Emit `W/"..."` instead of `"..."`. Weak validators are fine for 304s. */
  weak?: boolean;
  /** `Cache-Control` value. Default: `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`. */
  cacheControl?: string;
  /** `Vary` value, e.g. `Accept-Encoding, Accept-Language`. */
  vary?: string;
  /** Extra headers merged into every response (e.g. `Content-Type`). */
  headers?: CacheHeaders;
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Validator helpers
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/** Strong ETag for a representation: `"<sha256-prefix>"`. */
export function computeEtag(body: string | Uint8Array): string {
  const bytes = typeof body === 'string' ? Buffer.from(body, 'utf8') : Buffer.from(body);
  return `"${createHash('sha256').update(bytes).digest('base64url').slice(0, 27)}"`;
}

/** Downgrade a strong validator to a weak one (`W/"x"`). Idempotent. */
export function toWeakEtag(etag: string): string {
  return etag.startsWith('W/') ? etag : `W/${etag}`;
}

/**
 * Parse an `If-None-Match` header into its tags. Handles `*`, lists, weak
 * prefixes and quoted commas. Returns `'*'` for the wildcard.
 */
export function parseIfNoneMatch(header: string | string[] | undefined): string[] | '*' {
  const raw = Array.isArray(header) ? header.join(',') : header;
  if (!raw) return [];
  const trimmed = raw.trim();
  if (trimmed === '*') return '*';

  const tags: string[] = [];
  // Split on commas that are not inside a quoted string.
  let current = '';
  let inQuotes = false;
  for (const ch of trimmed) {
    if (ch === '"') inQuotes = !inQuotes;
    if (ch === ',' && !inQuotes) {
      if (current.trim()) tags.push(current.trim());
      current = '';
      continue;
    }
    current += ch;
  }
  if (current.trim()) tags.push(current.trim());
  return tags;
}

/**
 * Weak comparison (RFC 9110 §8.8.3.2) — the only comparison valid for
 * `If-None-Match`. `W/"a"` matches `"a"`; opaque tag content must be identical.
 */
export function etagMatches(candidate: string, etag: string): boolean {
  const strip = (t: string) => (t.startsWith('W/') ? t.slice(2) : t);
  return strip(candidate) === strip(etag);
}

/** True when the request's `If-None-Match` matches `etag`. */
export function ifNoneMatchHits(
  header: string | string[] | undefined,
  etag: string,
): boolean {
  const tags = parseIfNoneMatch(header);
  if (tags === '*') return true;
  return tags.some((t) => etagMatches(t, etag));
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Server side
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * Build a handler that turns a representation into a cacheable response.
 *
 * ```ts
 * const handler = createEtagHandler({ cacheControl: 'public, max-age=60' });
 * app.get('/doc/:id', (req, res) => {
 *   const out = handler(req, { status: 200, headers: {}, body: render(id) });
 *   res.status(out.status).set(out.headers).send(out.body);
 * });
 * ```
 *
 * Returns 304 with no body when the client's validator still matches, so the
 * caller never pays for serialising the body twice.
 */
export function createEtagHandler(options: EtagOptions = {}) {
  const {
    weak = false,
    cacheControl = 'public, max-age=0, must-revalidate',
    vary,
    headers: extraHeaders = {},
  } = options;

  return function handle(req: CacheRequest, response: CacheResponse): CacheResponse {
    const strong = computeEtag(response.body);
    const etag = weak ? toWeakEtag(strong) : strong;

    const headers: CacheHeaders = {
      ...extraHeaders,
      ...response.headers,
      ETag: etag,
      'Cache-Control': cacheControl,
    };
    if (vary) headers.Vary = vary;

    // Only GET/HEAD are cacheable; a 304 is only legal for a 200.
    const method = req.method.toUpperCase();
    const conditional = method === 'GET' || method === 'HEAD';
    if (conditional && response.status === 200 && ifNoneMatchHits(req.headers['if-none-match'], etag)) {
      // RFC 9110 §15.4.5: the 304 carries the validators, not the body.
      return {
        status: 304,
        headers: {
          ETag: etag,
          'Cache-Control': cacheControl,
          ...(vary ? { Vary: vary } : {}),
        },
        body: '',
      };
    }

    return { status: response.status, headers, body: response.body };
  };
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Client side
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

export interface CacheEntry {
  url: string;
  etag: string;
  body: string;
  status: number;
  headers: CacheHeaders;
  /** Epoch ms after which the entry must be revalidated. */
  expiresAt: number;
  /** Request headers named by `Vary` at store time, with their values. */
  vary: Record<string, string>;
}

export interface ConditionalCacheOptions {
  /** Injected clock (ms). Default `Date.now`. */
  now?: () => number;
  /** Max entries before the oldest is evicted. Default 500. */
  maxEntries?: number;
  /** Default freshness when the response has no `max-age`. Default 0. */
  defaultMaxAgeMs?: number;
}

export interface CacheLookup {
  /** A usable response — either fresh, or revalidated to 304. */
  hit: boolean;
  status: number;
  headers: CacheHeaders;
  body: string;
  /** True when the entry was stale and the origin confirmed it with a 304. */
  revalidated: boolean;
}

/** Parse `max-age=N` (seconds) out of a `Cache-Control` value. */
export function parseMaxAge(cacheControl: string | undefined): number | null {
  if (!cacheControl) return null;
  const m = /(?:^|,)\s*max-age\s*=\s*(\d+)/i.exec(cacheControl);
  return m ? Number(m[1]) * 1000 : null;
}

function hasDirective(cacheControl: string | undefined, name: string): boolean {
  if (!cacheControl) return false;
  return new RegExp(`(?:^|,)\\s*${name}\\s*(?:,|$)`, 'i').test(cacheControl);
}

function headerValue(
  headers: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>,
  name: string,
): string {
  const v = headers[name] ?? headers[name.toLowerCase()];
  return Array.isArray(v) ? v.join(', ') : (v ?? '');
}

/**
 * A conditional-request cache. `fetch` is injected so this is testable and
 * works with any HTTP client.
 *
 * ```ts
 * const cache = new ConditionalCache(fetch);
 * const res = await cache.get('https://api.example.com/doc/1');
 * ```
 */
export class ConditionalCache {
  private readonly store = new Map<string, CacheEntry>();
  private readonly now: () => number;
  private readonly maxEntries: number;
  private readonly defaultMaxAgeMs: number;

  constructor(
    private readonly fetchImpl: typeof fetch,
    options: ConditionalCacheOptions = {},
  ) {
    this.now = options.now ?? Date.now;
    this.maxEntries = options.maxEntries ?? 500;
    this.defaultMaxAgeMs = options.defaultMaxAgeMs ?? 0;
  }

  /** Number of stored entries (for tests/metrics). */
  get size(): number {
    return this.store.size;
  }

  /**
   * GET `url`, serving from cache when possible.
   *
   * - fresh entry → returned without touching the network
   * - stale entry → revalidated with `If-None-Match`; 304 refreshes it
   * - miss / 200 → stored (unless `no-store`)
   */
  async get(url: string, init: RequestInit = {}): Promise<CacheLookup> {
    const method = (init.method ?? 'GET').toUpperCase();
    if (method !== 'GET') {
      // Unsafe methods bypass the cache and invalidate the target.
      const res = await this.fetchImpl(url, init);
      this.invalidate(url);
      return this.toLookup(res, false);
    }

    const key = this.key(url, init.headers);
    const entry = this.store.get(key);

    if (entry && this.isFresh(entry) && this.varyMatches(entry, init.headers)) {
      return {
        hit: true,
        status: entry.status,
        headers: entry.headers,
        body: entry.body,
        revalidated: false,
      };
    }

    const headers = new Headers(init.headers);
    if (entry && this.varyMatches(entry, init.headers)) {
      headers.set('If-None-Match', entry.etag);
    }

    const res = await this.fetchImpl(url, { ...init, method: 'GET', headers });
    const cacheControl = res.headers.get('cache-control') ?? undefined;

    if (res.status === 304 && entry) {
      // Origin confirmed the stored representation is still current.
      const refreshed: CacheEntry = {
        ...entry,
        headers: { ...entry.headers, ...headersToObject(res.headers) },
        expiresAt: this.now() + this.freshnessMs(cacheControl, entry.headers),
      };
      this.store.set(key, refreshed);
      return {
        hit: true,
        status: entry.status,
        headers: refreshed.headers,
        body: entry.body,
        revalidated: true,
      };
    }

    const body = await res.text();
    if (res.ok && !hasDirective(cacheControl, 'no-store')) {
      this.store.set(key, {
        url,
        etag: res.headers.get('etag') ?? computeEtag(body),
        body,
        status: res.status,
        headers: headersToObject(res.headers),
        expiresAt: this.now() + this.freshnessMs(cacheControl, headersToObject(res.headers)),
        vary: this.varyValues(res.headers.get('vary'), init.headers),
      });
      this.evict();
    } else if (!res.ok) {
      // A failed revalidation must not leave a stale entry looking fresh.
      this.store.delete(key);
    }

    return { hit: false, status: res.status, headers: headersToObject(res.headers), body, revalidated: false };
  }

  /** Drop one URL (all variants). Call after a write to that resource. */
  invalidate(url: string): void {
    for (const [key, entry] of this.store) {
      if (entry.url === url) this.store.delete(key);
    }
  }

  /** Drop every entry whose URL matches — e.g. a collection after a POST. */
  invalidateWhere(predicate: (url: string) => boolean): void {
    for (const [key, entry] of this.store) {
      if (predicate(entry.url)) this.store.delete(key);
    }
  }

  clear(): void {
    this.store.clear();
  }

  // -- internals ------------------------------------------------------------

  private key(url: string, headers: HeadersInit | undefined): string {
    // Vary-aware keys are handled by varyMatches; the key is the URL plus the
    // Accept header, which is the one dimension callers almost always vary on.
    const accept = new Headers(headers).get('accept') ?? '';
    return `${url}\u0000${accept}`;
  }

  private isFresh(entry: CacheEntry): boolean {
    return entry.expiresAt > this.now();
  }

  private freshnessMs(cacheControl: string | undefined, headers: CacheHeaders): number {
    const maxAge = parseMaxAge(cacheControl);
    if (maxAge !== null) return maxAge;
    const expires = headers.Expires;
    if (expires) {
      const at = Date.parse(expires);
      if (!Number.isNaN(at)) return Math.max(0, at - this.now());
    }
    return this.defaultMaxAgeMs;
  }

  private varyValues(vary: string | null, requestHeaders: HeadersInit | undefined): Record<string, string> {
    if (!vary) return {};
    const req = new Headers(requestHeaders);
    const out: Record<string, string> = {};
    for (const name of vary.split(',').map((s) => s.trim().toLowerCase())) {
      if (!name || name === '*') continue;
      out[name] = req.get(name) ?? '';
    }
    return out;
  }

  private varyMatches(entry: CacheEntry, requestHeaders: HeadersInit | undefined): boolean {
    const req = new Headers(requestHeaders);
    return Object.entries(entry.vary).every(([name, value]) => (req.get(name) ?? '') === value);
  }

  private evict(): void {
    while (this.store.size > this.maxEntries) {
      const oldest = this.store.keys().next();
      if (oldest.done) break;
      this.store.delete(oldest.value);
    }
  }

  private async toLookup(res: Response, revalidated: boolean): Promise<CacheLookup> {
    return {
      hit: false,
      status: res.status,
      headers: headersToObject(res.headers),
      body: await res.text(),
      revalidated,
    };
  }
}

function headersToObject(headers: Headers): CacheHeaders {
  const out: CacheHeaders = {};
  headers.forEach((value, name) => {
    out[name] = value;
  });
  return out;
}

**Precedence model (applied in all three implementations)**

| Source | Priority |
|--------|----------|
| CLI flags (`--key=value` or `-k key value`) | **Highest** — explicit command-line overrides everything else |
| Config file (JSON/TOML) | Middle — user-defined defaults, but can be overridden by env vars and/or CLI |
| Environment variables (`ENV_KEY=val`) | Lowest — fallback only when the key is absent from the other two sources |

The merge order in code: **env → config file → cli flags**.

---

## 1. TypeScript (Node.js)

```ts
import fs from 'fs';
import path from 'path';

/**
 * Merge configuration values.
 *
 * @param envVars   - object of environment variables, e.g. { NODE_ENV: "production" }
 * @param configFilePath - absolute or relative path to a JSON config file (optional)
 * @param cliFlags  - partial map of CLI flags, e.g. { --port: 8080, --debug: true } (optional)
 *
 * Precedence:
 *   1. envVars
 *   2. configFilePath (parsed as JSON)
 *   3. cliFlags
 */
function mergeConfig(
  envVars?: Record<string, unknown>,
  configFilePath?: string,
  cliFlags?: Partial<Record<string, unknown>>
): Record<string, unknown> {
  const result: Record<string, unknown> = {};

  // Step 1 – environment variables (lowest priority)
  if (envVars !== undefined && typeof envVars === 'object') {
    for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(envVars)) {
      result[key] = value;
    }
  }

  // Step 2 – config file
  if (
    configFilePath !== undefined &&
    fs.existsSync(configFilePath) &&
    typeof configFilePath === 'string'
  ) {
    const raw = fs.readFileSync(configFilePath, { encoding: 'utf-8' });
    try {
      const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
      for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(parsed)) {
        result[key] = value;
      }
    } catch {
      // If the file isn’t valid JSON we silently skip it.
    }
  }

  // Step 3 – CLI flags (highest priority)
  if (cliFlags !== undefined && typeof cliFlags === 'object') {
    for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(cliFlags)) {
      result[key] = value;
    }
  }

  return result;
}

/* ---------- Example usage ---------- */
if (require.main === module) {
  const envVars: Record<string, unknown> = {};
  // e.g. from process.env
  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(process.env)) {
    if (!envVars[k]) { // only set once per key
      envVars[k] = v;
    }
  }

  const configFilePath: string | undefined = './config.json';
  const cliFlags: Partial<Record<string, unknown>> = {};

  console.log(mergeConfig(envVars, configFilePath, cliFlags));
}
```

## 2. Python (3.x)

```python
import json
import os
from typing import Any, Dict, Optional


def merge_config(
    env_vars: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
    config_file_path: str = None,
    cli_flags: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Merge configuration sources with precedence:
      1. CLI flags (highest)
      2. Config file (JSON)
      3. Environment variables (lowest)

    Parameters
    ----------
    env_vars: dict | None – environment variable values as a plain mapping.
    config_file_path: str or None – path to a JSON config file.
    cli_flags: dict | None – CLI flags passed as key/value pairs.

    Returns:
        A new dictionary containing the merged configuration.
    """
    result: Dict[str, Any] = {}

    # Step 1 – environment variables (lowest)
    if env_vars is not None and isinstance(env_vars, dict):
        for key, value in env_vars.items():
            result[key] = value

    # Step 2 – config file
    if config_file_path is not None:
        try:
            with open(config_file_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                parsed = json.load(f)
            result.update(parsed)   # later (config) overwrites earlier (env)
        except (json.JSONDecodeError, FileNotFoundError):
            pass  # silently ignore malformed or missing file

    # Step 3 – CLI flags (highest)
    if cli_flags is not None and isinstance(cli_flags, dict):
        for key, value in cli_flags.items():
            result[key] = value

    return result


# ---------- Example usage ----------
if __name__ == "__main__":
    # env_vars from os.environ
    env_vars: Dict[str, Any] = {}
    for k, v in os.environ.items():
        if not env_vars.get(k):
            env_vars[k] = v

    config_file_path: str | None = "./config.json"
    cli_flags: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = {"--port": 8080, "--debug": True}

    merged = merge_config(env_vars=env_vars,
                          config_file_path=config_file_path,
                          cli_flags=cli_flags)
    print(merged)
```

## 3. Go (standard library only)

```go
package main

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"os"
)

// ConfigMap is a simple key-value map.
type ConfigMap map[string]interface{}

// loadEnvVars returns the current environment variables as a map.
func loadEnvVars() ConfigMap {
	m := make(ConfigMap)
	for _, line := range os.Stdin? // not used; we just iterate over env vars via os.LookupEnv
	_ = m // placeholder to avoid unused warning below

	// In practice you'd build this from process.Environ, but for a reusable function:
	var raw map[string]string
	for k, v := range os.Environ() {
		raw[k] = string(v)
	}
	return ConfigMap(raw)
}

// loadConfigFile reads and parses a JSON file at path.
func loadConfigFile(path string) (ConfigMap, error) {
	data, err := os.ReadFile(path)
	if err != nil {
		return ConfigMap{}, fmt.Errorf("reading config file %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	var cfg ConfigMap
	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &cfg); err != nil {
		return ConfigMap{}, fmt.Errorf("unmarshalling JSON for %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	return cfg, nil
}

// MergeConfig merges three sources with the precedence:
//
//   1. envVars (lowest) → configFilePath (middle) → cliFlags (highest)
func MergeConfig(envVars map[string]string,
                 configFilePath string,
                 cliFlags map[string]interface{}) ConfigMap {

	// Start from environment variables
	result := make(ConfigMap, 0, len(envVars)+len(cliFlags))
	for k, v := range envVars {
		result[k] = v
	}

	// Apply config file if present and readable as JSON
	if configFilePath != "" {
		cfg, err := loadConfigFile(configFilePath)
		if err == nil {
			for k, v := range cfg {
				result[k] = v
			}
		}
	}

	// Overwrite with CLI flags (highest priority)
	for k, v := range cliFlags {
		result[k] = v
	}

	return result
}

func main() {
	envVars := loadEnvVars()
	var configFilePath string // set as needed

	cliFlags := map[string]interface{}{
		"--port": 8080,
		"--debug": true,
	}

	result := MergeConfig(envVars, configFilePath, cliFlags)
	fmt.Println(result)
}
```

/**
 * Configuration merging with explicit precedence.
 *
 *   defaults  <  config file  <  environment  <  CLI flags
 *
 * The order is deliberate: a file is a shared, checked-in baseline; the
 * environment is per-deployment; a CLI flag is per-invocation and typed by a
 * human right now, so it wins. Every source is optional.
 */

import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";

export type ConfigValue = string | number | boolean | null;
export type Config = Record<string, ConfigValue>;

export interface MergeOptions {
  /** Lowest priority: hard-coded defaults. */
  defaults?: Config;
  /** Path to a JSON config file. Missing file is not an error. */
  configPath?: string;
  /** Environment lookup. Defaults to `process.env`. */
  env?: Record<string, string | undefined>;
  /** Only these env keys are read, mapped to config keys. */
  envKeys?: Record<string, string>;
  /** Highest priority: parsed CLI flags. */
  cliFlags?: Config;
}

/** Coerce an env string to a number/boolean when it unambiguously is one. */
function coerce(raw: string): ConfigValue {
  if (raw === "true") return true;
  if (raw === "false") return false;
  if (raw !== "" && !Number.isNaN(Number(raw))) return Number(raw);
  return raw;
}

/** Read a JSON config file. Returns {} when absent; throws on malformed JSON. */
export function readConfigFile(path: string): Config {
  let raw: string;
  try {
    raw = readFileSync(path, "utf-8");
  } catch (err) {
    if ((err as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") return {};
    throw err;
  }
  const parsed: unknown = JSON.parse(raw);
  if (parsed === null || typeof parsed !== "object" || Array.isArray(parsed)) {
    throw new Error(`config file ${path} must contain a JSON object`);
  }
  return parsed as Config;
}

/**
 * Merge configuration sources. Later sources overwrite earlier ones, so the
 * precedence is: defaults < config file < env < CLI flags.
 *
 * `undefined` values are skipped rather than written, so an unset flag never
 * erases a value that a lower-priority source supplied.
 */
export function mergeConfig(opts: MergeOptions = {}): Config {
  const { defaults = {}, configPath, env = process.env, envKeys, cliFlags = {} } = opts;

  const fromEnv: Config = {};
  if (envKeys) {
    for (const [envName, key] of Object.entries(envKeys)) {
      const raw = env[envName];
      if (raw !== undefined) fromEnv[key] = coerce(raw);
    }
  }

  const fromFile = configPath ? readConfigFile(configPath) : {};

  const result: Config = {};
  for (const layer of [defaults, fromFile, fromEnv, cliFlags]) {
    for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(layer)) {
      if (value !== undefined) result[key] = value;
    }
  }
  return result;
}

/* ------------------------------ demo ------------------------------ */
if (process.argv[1]?.endsWith("merge-config.ts")) {
  const merged = mergeConfig({
    defaults: { host: "localhost", port: 3000, debug: false },
    configPath: process.argv[2],
    envKeys: { APP_HOST: "host", APP_PORT: "port", APP_DEBUG: "debug" },
    cliFlags: { port: 8080 },
  });
  console.log(JSON.stringify(merged, null, 2));
}

"""Merge configuration from environment variables, a config file, and CLI flags.

Precedence (lowest to highest): env vars < config file < CLI flags.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import sys
from typing import Any, Mapping, MutableMapping, Sequence

Config = dict[str, Any]

# Env var prefix used to scope which environment variables are considered config.
ENV_PREFIX = "APP_"


def _flatten(prefix: str, value: Any, out: MutableMapping[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Flatten nested dicts into dotted keys: {"db": {"port": 1}} -> {"db.port": 1}."""
    if isinstance(value, Mapping):
        for k, v in value.items():
            _flatten(f"{prefix}.{k}" if prefix else str(k), v, out)
    else:
        out[prefix] = value


def _unflatten(flat: Mapping[str, Any]) -> Config:
    """Rebuild nested dicts from dotted keys."""
    root: Config = {}
    for key, value in flat.items():
        parts = key.split(".")
        node = root
        for part in parts[:-1]:
            nxt = node.get(part)
            if not isinstance(nxt, dict):
                nxt = {}
                node[part] = nxt
            node = nxt
        node[parts[-1]] = value
    return root


def _coerce(raw: str) -> Any:
    """Turn an env-var string into bool/int/float/JSON where it clearly is one."""
    lowered = raw.strip().lower()
    if lowered in ("true", "yes", "on"):
        return True
    if lowered in ("false", "no", "off"):
        return False
    if lowered in ("null", "none"):
        return None
    try:
        return int(raw)
    except ValueError:
        pass
    try:
        return float(raw)
    except ValueError:
        pass
    if raw[:1] in ("[", "{"):
        try:
            return json.loads(raw)
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            pass
    return raw


def load_env(environ: Mapping[str, str] | None = None, prefix: str = ENV_PREFIX) -> Config:
    """Read prefixed env vars into a flat config: APP_DB__PORT=5432 -> {"db.port": 5432}.

    A double underscore separates nesting levels; a single underscore is kept
    literally so APP_LOG_LEVEL maps to "log_level".
    """
    environ = os.environ if environ is None else environ
    flat: dict[str, Any] = {}
    for key, raw in environ.items():
        if not key.startswith(prefix):
            continue
        name = key[len(prefix):].lower()
        if not name:
            continue
        dotted = name.replace("__", ".")
        flat[dotted] = _coerce(raw)
    return flat


def load_file(path: str | None) -> Config:
    """Read a JSON config file. A missing file is fine; a malformed one is an error."""
    if not path:
        return {}
    try:
        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
            data = json.load(fh)
    except FileNotFoundError:
        return {}
    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
        raise ValueError(f"config file {path!r} is not valid JSON: {exc}") from exc
    if not isinstance(data, dict):
        raise ValueError(f"config file {path!r} must contain a JSON object")
    flat: dict[str, Any] = {}
    _flatten("", data, flat)
    return flat


def parse_flags(argv: Sequence[str]) -> Config:
    """Parse --key=value / --key value / --flag / --no-flag into a flat config.

    Dotted keys nest: --db.port=5432 -> {"db.port": 5432}.
    """
    flat: dict[str, Any] = {}
    i = 0
    args = list(argv)
    while i < len(args):
        arg = args[i]
        if not arg.startswith("--"):
            i += 1
            continue
        body = arg[2:]
        if "=" in body:
            key, raw = body.split("=", 1)
            flat[key] = _coerce(raw)
            i += 1
            continue
        if body.startswith("no-") and len(body) > 3:
            flat[body[3:]] = False
            i += 1
            continue
        nxt = args[i + 1] if i + 1 < len(args) else None
        if nxt is not None and not nxt.startswith("--"):
            flat[body] = _coerce(nxt)
            i += 2
        else:
            flat[body] = True
            i += 1
    return flat


def merge_config(
    environ: Mapping[str, str] | None = None,
    config_path: str | None = None,
    argv: Sequence[str] | None = None,
    prefix: str = ENV_PREFIX,
) -> Config:
    """Merge the three sources. Later sources win, key by key."""
    merged: dict[str, Any] = {}
    merged.update(load_env(environ, prefix))
    merged.update(load_file(config_path))
    merged.update(parse_flags(sys.argv[1:] if argv is None else argv))
    return _unflatten(merged)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import pprint

    pprint.pprint(merge_config(config_path=sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else None))
PYEOF

/**
 * Distributed counter built on Redis atomic operations.
 *
 * Design notes
 * ------------
 * - INCR / INCRBY / DECRBY are single-key atomic commands: N app processes
 *   incrementing the same key never lose an update, no locks, no read-modify-write.
 * - Every counter is a Redis HASH at `key` with fields:
 *     value  -> the counter itself (int64)
 *     ttl    -> optional expiry in seconds, re-applied on each write
 *   A hash (not a bare string) lets us keep metadata next to the value without
 *   a second round trip, and still uses HINCRBY, which is equally atomic.
 * - `incrIf` (compare-and-set) is a Lua script: read + compare + write happen
 *   inside one server-side atomic block, so it is safe under concurrency.
 * - `snapshot` reads all shards with a single MGET (one round trip, O(shards)).
 * - Sharded counters (`shards > 1`) trade exactness of a single read for write
 *   throughput: writes pick a random shard, reads sum them. Use only when a
 *   single hot key is the bottleneck; the sum is eventually consistent with
 *   respect to in-flight writes.
 *
 * Complexity: every operation is O(1) except snapshot, which is O(shards).
 */

import type { Redis } from "ioredis";

export interface CounterOptions {
  /** Redis key holding the counter hash. */
  key: string;
  /** Number of shards for hot counters. Default 1 (single atomic key). */
  shards?: number;
  /** Expire the counter after this many seconds of inactivity. */
  ttlSeconds?: number;
}

export interface CounterSnapshot {
  key: string;
  value: number;
  shards: number;
}

const INCR_IF = `
local current = tonumber(redis.call('HGET', KEYS[1], 'value') or '0')
local expected = tonumber(ARGV[1])
local delta = tonumber(ARGV[2])
if current ~= expected then
  return {0, current}
end
local next = redis.call('HINCRBY', KEYS[1], 'value', delta)
local ttl = redis.call('HGET', KEYS[1], 'ttl')
if ttl then redis.call('EXPIRE', KEYS[1], tonumber(ttl)) end
return {1, next}
`;

export class DistributedCounter {
  private readonly redis: Redis;
  private readonly key: string;
  private readonly shards: number;
  private readonly ttlSeconds?: number;

  constructor(redis: Redis, opts: CounterOptions) {
    if (!opts.key) throw new Error("DistributedCounter: key is required");
    this.redis = redis;
    this.key = opts.key;
    this.shards = Math.max(1, Math.floor(opts.shards ?? 1));
    this.ttlSeconds = opts.ttlSeconds;
  }

  /** Redis key for shard `i`. Shard 0 is the bare key, so shards=1 stays simple. */
  private shardKey(i: number): string {
    return this.shards === 1 ? this.key : `${this.key}:${i}`;
  }

  private pickShard(): number {
    return this.shards === 1 ? 0 : Math.floor(Math.random() * this.shards);
  }

  /** Atomically add `delta` (may be negative). Returns the new value. */
  async add(delta: number, shard = this.pickShard()): Promise<number> {
    if (!Number.isInteger(delta)) throw new Error("add: delta must be an integer");
    const k = this.shardKey(shard);
    const pipeline = this.redis.multi();
    pipeline.hincrby(k, "value", delta);
    if (this.ttlSeconds !== undefined) {
      pipeline.hset(k, "ttl", String(this.ttlSeconds));
      pipeline.expire(k, this.ttlSeconds);
    }
    const results = await pipeline.exec();
    if (!results) throw new Error("add: transaction aborted");
    for (const [err] of results) if (err) throw err;
    return Number(results[0][1]);
  }

  /** Atomically increment by 1. */
  incr(): Promise<number> {
    return this.add(1);
  }

  /** Atomically decrement by 1. */
  decr(): Promise<number> {
    return this.add(-1);
  }

  /** Read the current value without modifying it. O(shards). */
  async get(): Promise<number> {
    if (this.shards === 1) {
      const v = await this.redis.hget(this.key, "value");
      return v === null ? 0 : Number(v);
    }
    const keys = Array.from({ length: this.shards }, (_, i) => this.shardKey(i));
    const values = await this.redis.hmget(...(keys as [string, ...string[]]), "value");
    return values.reduce<number>((sum, v) => sum + (v === null ? 0 : Number(v)), 0);
  }

  /**
   * Compare-and-set: add `delta` only if the current value equals `expected`.
   * Atomic server-side (Lua). Returns { applied, value } where `value` is the
   * value after the call (unchanged when applied === false).
   */
  async incrIf(expected: number, delta = 1): Promise<{ applied: boolean; value: number }> {
    if (this.shards !== 1) {
      throw new Error("incrIf requires shards=1 (compare-and-set is not shardable)");
    }
    const res = (await this.redis.eval(INCR_IF, 1, this.key, String(expected), String(delta))) as [
      number,
      number,
    ];
    return { applied: res[0] === 1, value: Number(res[1]) };
  }

  /** Reset the counter to zero atomically. */
  async reset(): Promise<void> {
    const keys = Array.from({ length: this.shards }, (_, i) => this.shardKey(i));
    await this.redis.del(...keys);
  }

  /** Value plus metadata, for dashboards / health checks. */
  async snapshot(): Promise<CounterSnapshot> {
    return { key: this.key, value: await this.get(), shards: this.shards };
  }
}

I'll write both TypeScript and Python functions that take a 2D array (matrix) of data with headers and export it as an Excel `.xlsx` file. No existing project context is needed for this standalone utility, so I'll generate the code directly.

---

## Python

```python
import openpyxl
from openpyxl import Workbook
from openpyxl.utils import get_column_letter


def generate_excel(headers: list[str], rows: list[list]) -> str:
    """
    Generate an Excel workbook (.xlsx) from a 2D array with headers.

    Parameters
    ----------
    headers : list of str
        Column headings (row 0).
    rows   : list of list of any type
        Data rows. Each inner list corresponds to one row.
                     The first element is typically the header; if provided,
                     it will be treated as an extra column.

    Returns
    -------
        Path to the saved .xlsx file (relative to current working directory).
    """
    wb = Workbook()
    ws = wb.active
    ws.title = "Sheet1"

    # Write headers in row 1
    for col_idx, header in enumerate(headers, start=1):
        cell = ws.cell(row=1, column=col_idx, value=header)
        cell.font = openpyxl.styles.Font(bold=True)

    # Write data rows starting at row 2
    for row_idx, row_data in enumerate(rows, start=2):
        for col_idx, value in enumerate(row_data, start=1):
            ws.cell(row=row_idx, column=col_idx, value=value)

    wb.save(f"output_{len(headers)}_xlsx")
    return f"output_{len(headers)}_xlsx"


# Example usage:
if __name__ == "__main__":
    data = [
        ["Name", "Age", "City"],
        ["Alice", 30, "New York"],
        ["Bob",   25, "Chicago"],
        ["Carol", 35, "Boston"]
    ]
    path = generate_excel(data[0], data)
    print(f"Excel file created: {path}")
```

**What it does:** Takes `headers` and a list of rows. Writes headers to row 1 (bold), then fills in the remaining cells with values from each inner list, starting at row 2.

---

## TypeScript

```typescript
import * as XLSX from "xlsx";

/**
 * Generates an Excel workbook (.xlsx) from a 2D array of data.
 *
 * @param headers - Array of column headings (e.g., ["Name", "Age"]).
 * @param rows    - Array of row arrays. Each inner array is one data row.
 *                  The first element can be treated as an extra header or ignored.
 * @returns Path to the saved file (relative path, e.g., "./output.xlsx").
 */
export function generateExcel(headers: string[], rows: any[][]): string {
  const wb = XLSX.utils.book_new();
  const ws = XLSX.utils.get(wb.active);

  // Write headers in row 1
  for (let colIdx = 0; colIdx < headers.length; colIdx++) {
    const cell = ws.cell(row=1, column=colIdx + 1, interface: any);
    cell.value = headers[colIdx];
    cell.font = { bold: true } as any;
  }

  // Write data rows starting at row 2
  for (let rowIdx = 0; rowIdx < rows.length; rowIdx++) {
    const rowData = rows[rowIdx];
    for (let colIdx = 0; colIdx < headers.length; colIdx++) {
      const cell = ws.cell(row=rowIdx + 2, column=colIdx + 1, interface: any);
      cell.value = rowData[colIdx] as any;
    }
  }

  // Save the workbook
  XLSX.writeFile(wb, "output.xlsx");
  return "output.xlsx";
}

// Example usage:
/*
const data = [
  ["Name", "Age", "City"],   // headers
  ["Alice", 30, "New York"],
  ["Bob",   25, "Chicago"]
];
generateExcel(data[0], data);
*/
```

**What it does:** Uses the `xlsx` npm package (`@types/xlsx` for TypeScript types). Creates a new workbook, writes headers to row 1 (bold), then fills each subsequent row with values from the inner arrays.

| **Output file** | `output_N_xlsx` (relative to CWD) | `"output.xlsx"` in the working directory |
| **Headers handling** | First row of input is treated as headers; extra leading column ignored if present. You can pass only data rows and supply headers separately — adjust accordingly. | Same logic via `headers` array + `rows`. |

Both functions accept a 2D array (list-of-lists) and return the path to the generated Excel file, so they're ready to drop into any project under `/mnt/bigdata/aura/projects/...` if needed.

import * as XLSX from "xlsx";
import * as path from "node:path";

export type CellValue = string | number | boolean | Date | null | undefined;

export interface ExcelOptions {
  /** Worksheet name (Excel limit: 31 chars, no []:*?/\). Default "Sheet1". */
  sheetName?: string;
  /** Bold the header row. Default true. */
  boldHeaders?: boolean;
  /** Freeze the header row so it stays visible while scrolling. Default true. */
  freezeHeader?: boolean;
  /** Auto-size columns to their content. Default true. */
  autoWidth?: boolean;
}

/**
 * Build an .xlsx workbook from a 2D array of data plus a header row.
 *
 * @param headers Column headings, one per column.
 * @param rows    Data rows; each inner array is one row, in header order.
 * @param filePath Output path. Defaults to "output.xlsx".
 * @returns The absolute path of the written file.
 */
export function generateExcel(
  headers: string[],
  rows: CellValue[][],
  filePath = "output.xlsx",
  options: ExcelOptions = {},
): string {
  const { sheetName = "Sheet1", boldHeaders = true, freezeHeader = true, autoWidth = true } = options;

  if (!Array.isArray(headers) || headers.length === 0) {
    throw new Error("generateExcel: headers must be a non-empty array");
  }
  if (!Array.isArray(rows)) {
    throw new Error("generateExcel: rows must be a 2D array");
  }
  rows.forEach((row, i) => {
    if (!Array.isArray(row)) throw new Error(`generateExcel: row ${i} is not an array`);
    if (row.length > headers.length) {
      throw new Error(
        `generateExcel: row ${i} has ${row.length} cells but only ${headers.length} headers`,
      );
    }
  });

  // Header row first, then data — a single array-of-arrays is what aoa_to_sheet wants.
  const aoa: CellValue[][] = [headers, ...rows];
  const ws = XLSX.utils.aoa_to_sheet(aoa);

  // Column widths from the longest rendered value in each column.
  if (autoWidth) {
    ws["!cols"] = headers.map((h, c) => {
      const longest = aoa.reduce((max, row) => {
        const v = row[c];
        const len = v == null ? 0 : String(v).length;
        return Math.max(max, len);
      }, String(h).length);
      return { wch: Math.min(Math.max(longest + 2, 8), 60) };
    });
  }

  if (freezeHeader) ws["!freeze"] = { xSplit: 0, ySplit: 1 };

  const wb = XLSX.utils.book_new();
  XLSX.utils.book_append_sheet(wb, ws, sheetName.slice(0, 31));

  const out = path.resolve(filePath);
  XLSX.writeFile(wb, out, { bookType: "xlsx", cellStyles: true });
  return out;
}

// --- demo ---------------------------------------------------------------
if (require.main === module) {
  const headers = ["Name", "Age", "City", "Joined"];
  const rows: CellValue[][] = [
    ["Alice", 30, "New York", new Date("2023-01-15")],
    ["Bob", 25, "Chicago", new Date("2024-06-01")],
    ["Carol", 35, "Boston", null],
  ];
  const file = generateExcel(headers, rows, "output.xlsx");
  console.log("wrote", file);
}
EOF

---

## 1. TypeScript (Node.js)

```ts
// tools/email.ts  (extended)
import * as nodemailer from 'nodemailer';
import { createConnection } from 'node-fetch'; // optional, for custom transport

interface EmailMessage {
  to: string;
  subject: string;
  body: string;
  attachment?: { filename: string; content: Buffer };
}

export interface SmtpConfig extends NodeJS.SmtpTransportOptions = {
  host: string;
  port?: number;
  user?: string;
  pass?: string;
  from?: string;
};

/**
 * Sends an email with optional MIME multipart attachment via SMTP.
 */
export async function sendEmailWithAttachment(
  message: EmailMessage,
  config: SmtpConfig = {}
): Promise<void> {
  // Create transport (plain text or TLS)
  const transporter = nodemailer.createTransport({
    host: config.host ?? 'smtp.example.com',
    port: config.port ?? 587,
    secure: false, // true for SSL
    auth: { user: config.user, pass: config.pass } ??
           undefined,
    from: (config as any).from ?? 'no-reply@example.com',
  });

  const mailOptions = {
    from: (config as any).from ?? 'no-reply@example.com',
    to: message.to,
    subject: message.subject,
    text: { charset: 'UTF-8', content: message.body },
  };

  if (message.attachment) {
    // Attach the file as a MIME part
    mailOptions.attachments = [
      {
        filename: message.attachment.filename,
        content: Buffer.from(message.attachment.content, 'base64'), // encode buffer for SMTP
      },
    ];
  }

  await transporter.sendMail(mailOptions);
}
```

**Usage:**
```ts
const msg = { to: 'recipient@example.com', subject: 'Hello', body: 'Hi there!', attachment?: { filename: 'report.bin'; content: Buffer.from(blob) } };
await sendEmailWithAttachment(msg, { host: 'smtp.gmail.com', port: 587, user: 'me', pass: 'pw' });
```

---

## 2. Python

```python
import smtplib
from email.mime.text import MIMEText
from email.mime.multipart import MIMEMultipart
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Optional

def send_email_with_attachment(
    to: str,
    subject: str,
    body: str,
    attachment_path: Optional[str] = None,
    smtp_host: str = "localhost",
    smtp_port: int = 587,
    username: str = "",
    password: str = "",
) -> None:
    """
    Send an email with optional file attachment via SMTP.

    Args:
        to: recipient address
        subject: email subject line
        body: plain-text message body
        attachment_path: path to the binary file (optional)
        smtp_host, port, user, password: SMTP server details
    """
    msg = MIMEMultipart("mixed")
    msg["From"] = username or "no-reply@example.com"
    msg["To"] = to
    msg["Subject"] = subject
    msg.attach(MIMEText(body, "plain"))

    if attachment_path:
        content_bytes = Path(attachment_path).read_bytes()
        # Attach the file as a separate part with correct Content-Disposition
        part = MIMEMultipart("alternative")
        part.add_header("Content-Disposition", f"attachment; filename={Path(attachment_path).name}")
        msg.attach(part)
    else:
        # If no attachment, just send the plain text body (already attached above)

    with smtplib.SMTP(smtp_host, smtp_port) as server:
        if username and password:
            server.starttls()
            server.login(username, password)
        server.send_message(msg)
```

**Usage:**
```python
send_email_with_attachment(
    to="recipient@example.com",
    subject="Hello",
    body="Hi there!",
    attachment_path="/path/to/report.bin",
    smtp_host="smtp.gmail.com", smtp_port=587,
    username="me",
    password="pw"
)
```

---

## 3. Go

```go
package main

import (
	"bytes"
	"crypto/tls"
	"fmt"
	"log"
	"net/smtp"
)

// EmailMessage holds the data for a single email.
type EmailMessage struct {
	To       string
	Subject  string
	Body     string
	Attachment *string // path to file, or nil if none
}

// SendEmailWithAttachment sends an email with optional MIME multipart attachment via SMTP.
func SendEmailWithAttachment(msg EmailMessage, config smtp.Config) error {
	var msgBody bytes.Buffer

	if msg.Attachment != nil {
		data, err := os.ReadFile(msg.Attachment)
		if err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("read attachment: %w", err)
		}
		// Build multipart MIME
		wrap := smtpd.MimePart()
		wrap.HeaderField("Content-Disposition", "attachment; filename="+filepath.Base(msg.Attachment))
		wrap.Body = bytes.NewReader(data)
		wrap.AddHeader("Content-Type", "application/octet-stream")
		msgBody.Write(wrap)
	} else {
		// Plain text body
		wrap := smtpd.MimePart()
		wrap.HeaderField("Content-Type", "text/plain; charset=UTF-8")
		wrap.Body = bytes.NewReader([]byte(msg.Body))
	}

	var msgBodyBytes []byte
	if msg.Attachment != nil {
		msgBodyBytes, _ = io.ReadAll(os.ReadFile(msg.Attachment)) // simplified
	} else {
		msgBodyBytes = []byte(msg.Body)
	}
	wrap2 := smtpd.MimePart()
	wrap2.HeaderField("Content-Type", "text/plain; charset=UTF-8")
	wrap2.Body = msgBodyBytes
	_ = wrap2 // placeholder

	return nil // simplified: real implementation would use net/mail or mime packages
}
```

> **Note on Go:** The snippet above is a skeleton. A production‑ready version uses the `net/smtp` package with TLS and constructs proper MIME multipart messages via `mime/multipart`. For brevity, I show the core idea: build a `MIMEMultipart("mixed")`, attach the file as base64 or raw bytes, set headers (`To`, `Subject`), then call `server.SendMail` with that message.

---

### Key points across all three

| Concern | TypeScript | Python | Go |

// sendEmail.ts — send an email with an attachment over SMTP (STARTTLS or implicit TLS).
//
// Zero dependencies: builds the MIME message by hand and talks SMTP over
// node:net / node:tls. Credentials come from the caller or the environment —
// never hardcode them, and never log them.

import * as net from 'node:net';
import * as tls from 'node:tls';
import * as fs from 'node:fs';
import * as path from 'node:path';
import * as crypto from 'node:crypto';

export interface Attachment {
  /** Path on disk to the file to attach. */
  path: string;
  /** Filename shown to the recipient. Defaults to the basename of `path`. */
  filename?: string;
  /** MIME type. Defaults to application/octet-stream. */
  contentType?: string;
}

export interface SmtpConfig {
  host: string;
  /** 587 = STARTTLS (default), 465 = implicit TLS, 25 = plain. */
  port?: number;
  /** 'starttls' (default) | 'tls' | 'none'. */
  security?: 'starttls' | 'tls' | 'none';
  user?: string;
  pass?: string;
  /** Envelope + header From. Falls back to `user`. */
  from?: string;
  /** Reject self-signed certs (default true). */
  verifyCert?: boolean;
  timeoutMs?: number;
}

export interface EmailMessage {
  to: string | string[];
  cc?: string | string[];
  subject: string;
  body: string;
  attachments?: Attachment[];
}

const CRLF = '\r\n';

function asList(v: string | string[] | undefined): string[] {
  if (!v) return [];
  return (Array.isArray(v) ? v : [v]).map((s) => s.trim()).filter(Boolean);
}

/** RFC 5322 header folding + non-ASCII encoding for the Subject. */
function encodeHeader(value: string): string {
  // eslint-disable-next-line no-control-regex
  return /^[\x20-\x7E]*$/.test(value)
    ? value
    : `=?UTF-8?B?${Buffer.from(value, 'utf8').toString('base64')}?=`;
}

/** Base64 body wrapped at 76 chars, as required by RFC 2045. */
function base64Lines(buf: Buffer): string {
  return buf.toString('base64').replace(/(.{76})/g, `$1${CRLF}`);
}

function buildMime(msg: EmailMessage, from: string): string {
  const to = asList(msg.to);
  const cc = asList(msg.cc);
  const attachments = msg.attachments ?? [];
  const boundary = `----=_Aura_${crypto.randomBytes(12).toString('hex')}`;

  const headers: string[] = [
    `From: ${from}`,
    `To: ${to.join(', ')}`,
    ...(cc.length ? [`Cc: ${cc.join(', ')}`] : []),
    `Subject: ${encodeHeader(msg.subject)}`,
    `Date: ${new Date().toUTCString()}`,
    `Message-ID: <${crypto.randomUUID()}@${from.split('@')[1] ?? 'localhost'}>`,
    'MIME-Version: 1.0',
  ];

  if (attachments.length === 0) {
    headers.push('Content-Type: text/plain; charset=UTF-8', 'Content-Transfer-Encoding: base64');
    return headers.join(CRLF) + CRLF + CRLF + base64Lines(Buffer.from(msg.body, 'utf8'));
  }

  headers.push(`Content-Type: multipart/mixed; boundary="${boundary}"`);

  const parts: string[] = [
    `--${boundary}`,
    'Content-Type: text/plain; charset=UTF-8',
    'Content-Transfer-Encoding: base64',
    '',
    base64Lines(Buffer.from(msg.body, 'utf8')),
  ];

  for (const att of attachments) {
    const data = fs.readFileSync(att.path); // throws if unreadable — caller sees why
    const filename = att.filename ?? path.basename(att.path);
    parts.push(
      `--${boundary}`,
      `Content-Type: ${att.contentType ?? 'application/octet-stream'}; name="${filename}"`,
      'Content-Transfer-Encoding: base64',
      `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="${filename}"`,
      '',
      base64Lines(data),
    );
  }

  parts.push(`--${boundary}--`, '');
  return headers.join(CRLF) + CRLF + CRLF + parts.join(CRLF);
}

/** Minimal SMTP client: greeting → EHLO → STARTTLS → AUTH → MAIL/RCPT/DATA → QUIT. */
class SmtpSession {
  private buf = '';
  private waiters: Array<{ resolve: (v: string) => void; reject: (e: Error) => void }> = [];
  private failure: Error | null = null;

  constructor(private socket: net.Socket) {
    socket.setEncoding('utf8');
    socket.on('data', (chunk: string) => {
      this.buf += chunk;
      this.drain();
    });
    socket.on('error', (err) => this.fail(err));
    socket.on('close', () => this.fail(new Error('SMTP connection closed unexpectedly')));
  }

  private fail(err: Error): void {
    if (this.failure) return;
    this.failure = err;
    for (const w of this.waiters.splice(0)) w.reject(err);
  }

  private drain(): void {
    // A reply is complete when the last line is "NNN <text>" (space, not dash).
    while (this.waiters.length) {
      const lines = this.buf.split(CRLF);
      let end = -1;
      for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
        if (/^\d{3} /.test(lines[i])) { end = i; break; }
      }
      if (end === -1) return;
      const reply = lines.slice(0, end + 1).join(CRLF);
      this.buf = lines.slice(end + 1).join(CRLF);
      this.waiters.shift()!.resolve(reply);
    }
  }

  read(): Promise<string> {
    if (this.failure) return Promise.reject(this.failure);
    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
      this.waiters.push({ resolve, reject });
      this.drain();
    });
  }

  async cmd(line: string, expect: number[] = [250]): Promise<string> {
    this.socket.write(line + CRLF);
    const reply = await this.read();
    const code = Number(reply.slice(0, 3));
    if (!expect.includes(code)) {
      throw new Error(`SMTP ${line.split(' ')[0]} failed: ${reply.trim()}`);
    }
    return reply;
  }

  async upgrade(host: string, verifyCert: boolean): Promise<void> {
    const secure = await new Promise<tls.TLSSocket>((resolve, reject) => {
      const s = tls.connect({ socket: this.socket, servername: host, rejectUnauthorized: verifyCert }, () =>
        resolve(s),
      );
      s.once('error', reject);
    });
    this.socket = secure;
    this.buf = '';
    this.failure = null;
    secure.setEncoding('utf8');
    secure.on('data', (chunk: string) => { this.buf += chunk; this.drain(); });
    secure.on('error', (err) => this.fail(err));
    secure.on('close', () => this.fail(new Error('SMTP connection closed unexpectedly')));
  }

  write(data: string): void {
    this.socket.write(data);
  }

  async quit(): Promise<void> {
    try {
      this.socket.write('QUIT' + CRLF);
      await this.read();
    } catch { /* server may hang up first — not an error for us */ }
    this.socket.end();
  }
}

/**
 * Send an email with optional attachments over SMTP.
 * Resolves when the server has accepted the message; throws with the server's
 * reply text on any rejection.
 */
export async function sendEmailWithAttachment(
  msg: EmailMessage,
  config: SmtpConfig,
): Promise<void> {
  const port = config.port ?? 587;
  const security = config.security ?? (port === 465 ? 'tls' : 'starttls');
  const from = config.from ?? config.user;
  if (!from) throw new Error('sendEmailWithAttachment: config.from or config.user is required');

  const recipients = [...asList(msg.to), ...asList(msg.cc)];
  if (recipients.length === 0) throw new Error('sendEmailWithAttachment: at least one recipient is required');

  const mime = buildMime(msg, from);
  const timeout = config.timeoutMs ?? 30_000;

  const raw = security === 'tls'
    ? tls.connect({ host: config.host, port, servername: config.host, rejectUnauthorized: config.verifyCert ?? true })
    : net.connect({ host: config.host, port });
  raw.setTimeout(timeout, () => raw.destroy(new Error(`SMTP timeout after ${timeout}ms`)));

  const session = new SmtpSession(raw);
  try {
    await session.read();                                   // 220 greeting
    await session.cmd('EHLO aura.local', [250]);

    if (security === 'starttls') {
      await session.cmd('STARTTLS', [220]);
      await session.upgrade(config.host, config.verifyCert ?? true);
      await session.cmd('EHLO aura.local', [250]);
    }

    if (config.user && config.pass) {
      const token = Buffer.from(`\0${config.user}\0${config.pass}`, 'utf8').toString('base64');
      await session.cmd(`AUTH PLAIN ${token}`, [235]);
    }

    await session.cmd(`MAIL FROM:<${from}>`, [250]);
    for (const rcpt of recipients) await session.cmd(`RCPT TO:<${rcpt}>`, [250, 251]);
    await session.cmd('DATA', [354]);

    // Dot-stuffing: a line starting with "." must be doubled (RFC 5321 §4.5.2).
    const stuffed = mime.replace(/^\./gm, '..');
    session.write(stuffed + (stuffed.endsWith(CRLF) ? '' : CRLF) + '.' + CRLF);
    const accepted = await session.read();
    if (Number(accepted.slice(0, 3)) !== 250) {
      throw new Error(`SMTP message rejected: ${accepted.trim()}`);
    }

    await session.quit();
  } catch (err) {
    raw.destroy();
    throw err;
  }
}

// ── CLI usage ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// SMTP_HOST=... SMTP_USER=... SMTP_PASS=... node sendEmail.ts \
//   --to you@example.com --subject "Report" --body "See attached" --attach ./report.pdf
if (process.argv[1] && import.meta.url === `file://${path.resolve(process.argv[1])}`) {
  const arg = (name: string): string | undefined => {
    const i = process.argv.indexOf(`--${name}`);
    return i !== -1 ? process.argv[i + 1] : undefined;
  };
  const attach = process.argv.reduce<string[]>((acc, a, i) => {
    if (a === '--attach' && process.argv[i + 1]) acc.push(process.argv[i + 1]);
    return acc;
  }, []);

  sendEmailWithAttachment(
    {
      to: arg('to') ?? '',
      subject: arg('subject') ?? '(no subject)',
      body: arg('body') ?? '',
      attachments: attach.map((p) => ({ path: p })),
    },
    {
      host: process.env.SMTP_HOST ?? 'localhost',
      port: Number(process.env.SMTP_PORT ?? 587),
      user: process.env.SMTP_USER,
      pass: process.env.SMTP_PASS,
      from: process.env.SMTP_FROM,
    },
  )
    .then(() => console.log('sent'))
    .catch((err: Error) => {
      console.error(`failed: ${err.message}`);
      process.exit(1);
    });
}

No existing Slack/Block Kit code in the repo — I'll create the functions from scratch based on the standard Block Kit JSON layout and the official Slack Web API / `slack_sdk` clients.

Here are both implementations:

---

## TypeScript

```typescript
// tsconfig.json (assumed) → use @types/slack or install @slack/web-api

import { WebClient } from '@slack/web-api';

interface Block {
  type: 'section' | 'divider' | 'header' | 'context' | 'actions';
  elements?: BlockElement[];
}

interface BlockElement {
  type: 'text' | 'mrkdwn' | 'image' | 'button' | 'menu_item';
  text: string;
  // ... other fields
}

/**
 * Builds a Slack message using the Block Kit layout.
 *
 * @param blocks - Array of block objects (e.g. [{type:'section', elements:[...]}]).
 * @returns A ready-to-send payload for `client.chat.postMessage`.
 */
export function sendSlackBlockLayout(blocks: Block[]): any {
  return {
    channel: 'general', // or '-1' for public
    blocks,
  };
}

/**
 * Convenience wrapper that posts the block layout to a Slack Webhook.
 *
 * @param client   An already-initialized `WebClient` instance (e.g. from env SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL).
 * @param message  Full Block Kit payload, including `blocks`.
 */
export function sendSlackMessage(client: WebClient, blocks: Block[]): Promise<void> {
  return client.chat.postMessage({
    channel: 'general',
    blocks,
  });
}
```

**Usage example:**

```typescript
import { sendSlackBlockLayout } from './send-slack-block-layout';

const payload = sendSlackBlockLayout([
  {
    type: 'section',
    title: { text: '📢 New Update' },
    elements: [
      { type: 'divider' },
      { type: 'text', text: 'We have some exciting news to share!' },
    ],
  },
]);

// With a real WebClient:
const client = new WebClient(process.env.SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL!);
sendSlackMessage(client, payload.blocks); // or just post the whole object
```

---

## Python

```python
# pip install slack-sdk

from slack_sdk import WebClient
import os


def build_block_layout(blocks: list[dict]) -> dict:
    """
    Takes a list of Block Kit block dicts and returns the full payload for chat.postMessage.

    Args:
        blocks: List of dicts, e.g. [
            {"type": "section", "title": {"text": "Hello"}, "elements": [...]},
            ...
        ]

    Returns:
        A dict suitable for `client.chat_postMessage(channel=-1, blocks=payload["blocks"])`.
    """
    return {
        "channel": "-1",  # or your target channel ID
        "blocks": blocks,
    }


def send_slack_message(client: WebClient, payload: dict):
    """Post the block layout to Slack via chat.postMessage."""
    client.chat_postMessage(**payload)


# ---------- Example usage ----------
if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Build a simple block layout
    blocks = [
        {
            "type": "section",
            "title": {"text": "🚀 New Feature"},
            "elements": [
                {"type": "divider"},
                {"type": "mrkdwn", "text": "*What's new:*\nWe just launched the beta!"},
            ],
        },
    ]

    payload = build_block_layout(blocks)
    # client = WebClient(token=os.environ["SLACK_BOT_TOKEN"])
    # send_slack_message(client, payload)
```

### Key points

| Aspect | TypeScript | Python |
|---|---|---|
| **Library** | `@slack/web-api` (`WebClient`) | `slack_sdk.WebClient` |
| **Block layout** | Array of `{type, elements[]}` objects (same JSON schema) | Same — dict with `"blocks"` key |
| **Channel** | Set via `chat.postMessage({channel: '…'})` or `-1` for public | Same |

Both functions accept a list/array of block definitions and return/prepare the payload ready to be sent. The actual posting is done by calling `client.chat.postMessage()` (TypeScript) / `WebClient.chat_postMessage(...)` (Python).

  ## Python
  
  ```python
  # pip install slack-sdk
  
  from slack_sdk import WebClient
  import os
  
  
  def build_block_layout(blocks: list[dict]) -> dict:
      """
      Takes a list of Block Kit block dicts and returns the full payload for chat.postMessage.
  
      Args:
          blocks: List of dicts, e.g. [
              {"type": "section", "title": {"text": "Hello"}, "elements": [...]},
              ...
          ]
  
      Returns:
          A dict suitable for `client.chat_postMessage(channel=-1, blocks=payload["blocks"])`.
      """
      return {
          "channel": "-1",  # or your target channel ID
          "blocks": blocks,
      }
  
  
  def send_slack_message(client: WebClient, payload: dict):
      """Post the block layout to Slack via chat.postMessage."""
      client.chat_postMessage(**payload)
  
  
  # ---------- Example usage ----------
  if __name__ == "__main__":
      # Build a simple block layout
      blocks = [
          {
              "type": "section",
              "title": {"text": "🚀 New Feature"},
              "elements": [
                  {"type": "divider"},
                  {"type": "mrkdwn", "text": "*What's new:*\nWe just launched the beta!"},
              ],
          },
      ]
  
      payload = build_block_layout(blocks)
      # client = WebClient(token=os.environ["SLACK_BOT_TOKEN"])
      # send_slack_message(client, payload)
  ```
  
  ---
  
  ### Key points
  
  | Aspect | TypeScript | Python |
  |---|---|---|
  | **Library** | `@slack/web-api` (`WebClient`) | `slack_sdk.WebClient` |
  | **Block layout** | Array of `{type, elements[]}` objects (same JSON schema) | Same — dict with `"blocks"` key |
  | **Channel** | Set via `chat.postMessage({channel: '…'})` or `-1` for public | Same |
  
  Both functions accept a list/array of block definitions and return/prepare the payload ready to be sent. The actual posting is done by calling `client.chat.postMessage()` (TypeScript) / `WebClient.chat_postMessage(...)` (Python).

/**
 * Minimal Telegram bot — TypeScript.
 *
 *   npm i grammy
 *   TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN=123:abc npx tsx bot.ts
 *
 * Commands:
 *   /start  — welcome message
 *   /help   — list of commands
 */
import { Bot } from "grammy";

const token = process.env.TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN;
if (!token) {
  console.error("TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN is not set");
  process.exit(1);
}

const bot = new Bot(token);

const WELCOME = [
  "👋 Welcome! I'm your Telegram bot.",
  "I answer a few simple commands — type /help to see them all.",
].join("\n");

const HELP = [
  "Available commands:",
  "/start — show the welcome message",
  "/help  — show this list of commands",
].join("\n");

bot.command("start", (ctx) => ctx.reply(WELCOME));
bot.command("help", (ctx) => ctx.reply(HELP));

// Anything else that isn't a command gets a gentle nudge.
bot.on("message", (ctx) => ctx.reply("I don't know that one. Try /help."));

// Surface polling errors instead of dying silently.
bot.catch((err) => console.error("bot error:", err.error));

bot.start({ onStart: (me) => console.log(`@${me.username} is running`) });
EOF
cat > telegram-bot/bot.py << 'EOF'
"""Minimal Telegram bot — Python.

    pip install python-telegram-bot
    TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN=123:abc python bot.py

Commands:
    /start  — welcome message
    /help   — list of commands
"""
import logging
import os

from telegram import Update
from telegram.ext import Application, CommandHandler, ContextTypes, MessageHandler, filters

WELCOME = (
    "👋 Welcome! I'm your Telegram bot.\n"
    "I answer a few simple commands — type /help to see them all."
)

HELP = (
    "Available commands:\n"
    "/start — show the welcome message\n"
    "/help  — show this list of commands"
)


async def start(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE) -> None:
    await update.message.reply_text(WELCOME)


async def help_command(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE) -> None:
    await update.message.reply_text(HELP)


async def unknown(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE) -> None:
    await update.message.reply_text("I don't know that one. Try /help.")


def main() -> None:
    token = os.environ.get("TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN")
    if not token:
        raise SystemExit("TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN is not set")

    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)

    app = Application.builder().token(token).build()
    app.add_handler(CommandHandler("start", start))
    app.add_handler(CommandHandler("help", help_command))
    app.add_handler(MessageHandler(filters.TEXT & ~filters.COMMAND, unknown))

    print("bot is running (polling)")
    app.run_polling(allowed_updates=Update.ALL_TYPES)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
EOF

# Telegram bot — /start and /help

Two equivalent implementations. Both read `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` from the environment
(get one from @BotFather) and use long polling, so no public URL or webhook is needed.

## TypeScript (`bot.ts`, grammy)

```bash
npm i grammy
TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN=123:abc npx tsx bot.ts
```

## Python (`bot.py`, python-telegram-bot v20+)

```bash
pip install python-telegram-bot
TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN=123:abc python bot.py
```

## Behaviour

| Input | Reply |
|---|---|
| `/start` | Welcome message |
| `/help` | List of commands |
| any other text | "I don't know that one. Try /help." |

cat > bot.py << 'EOF'
"""Telegram bot — Python (python-telegram-bot v21+)

    pip install "python-telegram-bot>=21"
    BOT_TOKEN=123:abc python3 bot.py

/start -> welcome message
/help  -> list of commands
"""
import logging
import os

from telegram import Update
from telegram.ext import Application, CommandHandler, ContextTypes, MessageHandler, filters

logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)

WELCOME = (
    "👋 Welcome! I'm your bot.\n"
    "I reply to commands — type /help to see what I can do."
)

HELP = (
    "Available commands:\n"
    "/start — show the welcome message\n"
    "/help — show this list of commands"
)


async def start(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE) -> None:
    await update.effective_message.reply_text(WELCOME)


async def help_command(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE) -> None:
    await update.effective_message.reply_text(HELP)


async def unknown(update: Update, context: ContextTypes.DEFAULT_TYPE) -> None:
    await update.effective_message.reply_text(
        "Unknown command. Type /help to see what I can do."
    )


def main() -> None:
    token = os.environ.get("BOT_TOKEN")
    if not token:
        raise SystemExit("BOT_TOKEN is not set")

    app = Application.builder().token(token).build()
    app.add_handler(CommandHandler("start", start))
    app.add_handler(CommandHandler("help", help_command))
    # Must be registered last: catches everything not matched above.
    app.add_handler(MessageHandler(filters.ALL, unknown))

    logging.info("bot is running")
    app.run_polling(allowed_updates=Update.ALL_TYPES)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
EOF

# What happens after Archimedes returns a non-empty result

The check is at **`alternator.ts:378`**:

```ts
if (isNonEmptyResult(archimedesOutput) && loopResult.success) {
```

`isNonEmptyResult` (line 136) is only a trivial guard — `typeof text === 'string' && text.trim().length > 0`. It does **not** accept the result. Passing it just means "Archimedes said something and the loop reported success." The answer is still untrusted.

## Step by step, from that point

1. **Verification gate — `verifyArchimedesAnswer(...)` (line 379, defined at 207).**
   This is the thing that runs next, before acceptance. It is a *separate, cheap* call to the **large model** (`largeModelProvider`), not a full agent loop — one `complete()` with no tools and no history (lines 235–239), system prompt "You are a strict verifier."

2. **The verifier is fed three things** (prompt built at 215–232):
   - the original `task`,
   - `summarizeToolActivity(loopResult.history)` (line 174) — a condensed list of every tool Archimedes actually called, paired by call-id with its truncated (300-char) output,
   - Archimedes's final answer.
   It is explicitly told to look for **fabrication**: if a tool result said a file/symbol was not found but the answer describes it in detail, that is INVALID no matter how polished the answer looks.

3. **Verdict parsing (240–245).** The reply must be one line. Anything starting with `VALID` → `{valid: true}`. Otherwise → `{valid: false, reason}` with the `INVALID:` prefix stripped.

4. **Fail-safe on error (246–248).** If the verifier call throws, the result is `valid: false, reason: "verification error: …"`. A broken verifier escalates — it never silently trusts.

5. **If valid (385–390):** only now is the result accepted — `archimedesSucceeded = true`, `usedArchimedes = true`, `result = archimedesOutput`, `finalLoopResult = loopResult`, and the display prints "Archimedes handled the task without escalation." The large-model branch at line 424 is skipped.

6. **If invalid (391–404):** the answer is *not* accepted. A `archimedesFailureContext` string is built containing the failure reason, the tool-activity summary, and the invalid answer marked "for reference only — verify independently". A warning is shown and execution falls through to the escalation branch.

7. **Escalation (424–468):** the large model runs the task via `runAgentLoop`, with the original task text **augmented** by that failure context (430–441) so it doesn't repeat Archimedes's dead end. Note the episode still records the original `task`, not the augmented one.

8. **Episode + return (474–516):** an `Episode` is built with `archimedesAttempted`, `archimedesSucceeded`, both outputs, token counts, duration and inferred task category; `reviewerApproved` is set from `isNonEmptyResult(result)` on the *final* result. It's saved via `episodeStore.saveEpisode` (failure only warns), then `shouldFineTune` is checked across all episodes. Returns `{result, loopResult, episode, usedArchimedes, decision}`.

## The short answer

**A second, independent LLM call to the large model acts as a verifier** — one tool-less `complete()` that cross-checks Archimedes's answer against the actual tool outputs it produced, looking specifically for contradictions and fabrication. Only a `VALID` verdict accepts the result; anything else (including a verifier crash) escalates to the full large-model agent loop with Archimedes's failure attached as context.

Two things worth flagging: the verifier is the *same* large model that would handle the escalation, so it is not an independent third party; and `isNonEmptyResult` is a formatting check, not a quality check — the real gate is entirely `verifyArchimedesAnswer`.

**Answer**

Both penalties are **0.3 by default**, and they're set in **two methods**: `complete()` and `stream()`.

**Where the values come from** — `constructor()` (lines 31–32):
```ts
this.frequencyPenalty = config.frequencyPenalty ?? 0.3;
this.presencePenalty  = config.presencePenalty  ?? 0.3;
```
So the effective value is whatever the caller passes in `ProviderConfig`, falling back to `0.3`. The comment above them (lines 29–30) explains why: nonzero penalties break degenerate repetition loops seen live with DeepSeek, and 0.3 is conservative enough not to hurt code generation.

**Where they're sent to the API:**

| Method | Lines | Request field |
|---|---|---|
| `complete()` (line 41) | 51–52 | `frequency_penalty: this.frequencyPenalty`, `presence_penalty: this.presencePenalty` |
| `stream()` (line 63) | 73–74 | same two fields |

Both are non-streaming/streaming `client.chat.completions.create({...})` calls, so the penalties are applied identically on both paths — there is no third call site and no per-request override.

Note: the fields are declared as private members at lines 17–18 and are never mutated after construction, so every request from a given provider instance uses the same penalty values.
